ParlaMint-PL_2018-04-12-sejm-61-2.u10	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The future EU budget should ensure the stable social, economic and territorial development of the European Union as a whole and must therefore have the necessary resources to address these traditional challenges and these new challenges and the Community objectives that we need to meet after 2020. It should be designed to ensure that these key issues are resolved from the perspective of the citizens of the European Union. This, of course, applies to many areas, which is why, in our opinion, the Polish government believes that cohesion policy should be maintained as a priority for the European Union. This scope should be wide and countries and regions should be able to adapt their public intervention to the deficits they have identified. From the point of view of Poland it will be another period of catching up on infrastructure delays. Priority will be given to the tasks of innovation in the economy and the creation of the basis for the 4.0 economy. Poland also intends to invest in clean air, clean energy, low energy intensity and resource consumption, as well as protection against the effects of climate change. We consider the issues of the labour market, adaptability, health, social inclusion, the fight against poverty and education to be extremely important social tasks. As part of our country's development needs in the strategy of responsible development, which is the basis for Poland's development directions, we have identified areas in the field of digitization, innovation and infrastructure, which are the priorities of the Ministry in the coming years. And so, in terms of increasing the innovation of the Polish economy, we plan to focus on: above all, support for the creation of innovation at every stage, including at the most risky stage of research and development, at the stage of the implementation of R &amp; D projects, at the stage of the presence of Polish products on global markets, the protection of intellectual property, technology transfer, development of networks of cooperation between companies, as well as scientific and business cooperation, and building such a potential for the absorption of knowledge in companies that will increase their level; support the development of innovative services; support for R &amp; D infrastructure; funding for Polish companies in the digital transformation of industry and transition to a circular economy model. As regards the construction of the digital economy, we plan in particular to support the development of telecommunications infrastructure, the 5G Internet as the basis for the operation of businesses and citizens in the Digital Single Market. We would like to strengthen the processes of providing data from the public sector and science collections to the economy in the context of big data, big data areas, as well as increasing efforts to boost demand for these services and cooperation in the construction and use of software created by multidisciplinary teams of analysts. We would like to increase the supply of investment for information and communication technologies in companies where we want the digital sector to be advanced and relevant and in the digital supply chain, and in the enhanced on-line trading panel of services and production. We want to develop digital e-government services and to interest entrepreneurs more in processes that protect management and cybersecurity of administrative systems. Finally, in terms of infrastructure alongside the construction of a high-quality core transport network, we will pay particular attention to solutions promoting clean air - this is, of course, primarily about public transport, the use of alternative means of powering this communication, as well as collective integration: common tickets, information systems, connecting nodes; clean energy - it is about preference for solutions that ensure maximum availability and which reflect local needs in terms of waste management and the use of local potential, promoting behaviour that favours the rational use of energy by end-users through the continuation of advisory support, also targeted at small operators, the housing sector, strengthening RES infrastructure to make greater use of green energy in Poland; clean water understood as access to a sufficient amount of good quality water, which is a condition for development; protection against flooding and drought - flood prevention infrastructure - complementary to investment for the development of inland waterways. These guidelines will be raised by Poland in the framework of the negotiations of the multiannual financial framework, i.e. already in May this year we will put these demands on the agenda of the common policy [[♪ The bell ♪]] in the area of cohesion policy beyond 2020.

Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, The Law of 13 November 2003 on the income of local self-government units was introduced on 1 January 2004. I will begin by answering the question directly to the Member about the funeral. Since the beginning of the provisions of the 2004 Social Assistance Act, the burial of a deceased person who lived in a social security home had no immediate family or no immediate family, belonged to a social assistance home, not to social services centres, especially since the social assistance house also receives a funeral allowance from the Social Insurance Institution to cover funeral expenses. At the same time, I would like to draw attention to the financing of social services homes. By the end of 2003, the residence at the social assistance home was financed by the county, which received a grant from the state budget. On the other hand, funding rules have changed since 2004. At the same time, the participation of municipalities with the obligation to finance the resident of the social assistance home has increased if the family does not do so. The share of the municipality in income tax on individuals was at that time, in 2003, 27.6% , and after the delegation of this task, this amount is increased annually and amounted to 37.9% in 2017 So we have an increase of about 10 percentage points compared to 2003. In addition, subsidies for DPS funding were reduced by 2015 on the basis of the Local Government Income Act due to the decreasing number of residents placed there on an old basis, i.e. to social assistance homes until 2004. Thus, in practice, the grant was reduced by an average of around 3% each year, e.g. in 2013 there was a reduction to approx. 43 million PLN. Starting in 2016, the measures planned for this purpose in the voivodship budgets have increased, which means that we no longer reduce this by the percentage of the population that we lose. In 2016 they increased by almost 23 million, in 2017 by about 6.5 million. In addition, the Minister for Family, Labour and Social Policy, as the responsibilities of the special reserve for social assistance tasks, shall, as far as possible, take action to increase the voivodeship's budgets in this chapter. Thus, in 2015 the voivod's budgets were increased by about PLN 13 million, in 2016 by about PLN 30 million and in 2017 by more than PLN 50 million. We are talking about all those residents who are placed there on an old basis, that is to say, we are talking about financing the current activities of the social services house as regards those residents who were placed there before 2004. This year we are also planning to go to the voivodships due to the demand for social services homes. ParlaMint-PL_2018-04-12-sejm-61-2.u60	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Thank you so much for that question. It is really at the heart of our actions and how we perceive energy security. I would like to thank you for the question related to Podkarpacie, because this region is indeed a key region for ensuring the energy security of Poland. Let me remind you that extraction from deposits in this region accounts for up to 36% the annual national extraction of natural gas. In Poland in 2017, the total production was about 4 billion m3, and what came to us from Podkarpacie is 1.4 billion m3, and an additional 5% annual domestic oil extraction, or around 40,000. tonnes of oil. At the same time, we are aware of a very large difference in how we are able to meet our needs in terms of gas and in terms of oil, in terms of domestic extraction. The Polish Petroleum Mining and Gas Industry Company in the south of Poland uses 136 oil and natural gas deposits and uses 2 000. wells. It is searching for new deposits, primarily with the aim of maintaining and increasing the production of hydrocarbons from the region in question, which, as I mentioned, is, of course, aimed at increasing the energy security of the State. We are aware that there is no safer source of gas than domestic extraction, and the company is constantly taking action to intensify extraction from indigenous deposits and to accelerate the process of developing already recognized deposits. Indeed, in the last few years the burden of searching has shifted clearly into the region of southern Poland. Indeed, the engineers and geologists mentioned by your Member who are working intensively in the region at this point see the greatest opportunities for development and discovery of new deposits. New knowledge, but also new technologies, are being submitted to a number of exploration projects in the Carpathians and the Pre-Góra to discover new gas resources in the coming years. As regards the Podkarpackie voivodship, the company has 22 concessions for exploration and exploration of hydrocarbons with a total area of 14 300 km2. On seven of them, PGNiG works together with Orlen Upstream, i.e. Bieszczady project, and in the framework of the operational work, seismic photographs have been taken together, search holes have been drilled, deposit tests have been carried out. A further three search holes and additional 2D seismic works were planned for the years 2018 and 2019. These search licenses of PGNiG and Orlen Upstream occupy almost 60% of the area of the Podkarpackie Voivodeship. In recent years, 4 billion m3 of gas has been documented, and the use of these new technologies has also allowed the discovery of unconventional deposits of Siedleczek, Kramarzówka, Chodakówka or Sądziszów. These deposits are already in the investment phase, and some of them started mining, which is happening e.g. On Siedleczek's deposit. In the very area of the activities of the PGNiG branch in Sanoku in 2016, 16 new holes were drilled and all were positive. In 2017, 22 bore holes 16 were positive. For the following years, we expect further increase of this effort in the field of drilling in Podkarpackie voivodship and in the Sanocki area, where further 24 openings are planned for the period 2019-2020, and in total in the 3-year plan PGNiG provides for drilling of about 80 holes in the south of Poland. 3D seismic works are carried out on an area of about 400 km2 and 2D seismic works on an area of about 160 km2, which will allow further extensification and design of new drillings in subsequent years. As far as this period is concerned, i.e. 2018,2020, PGNiG will allocate about PLN 1.8 billion for the implementation of exploration and exploration works in the southern part of Poland, the development of new deposits, as well as the expansion of mining and modernisation of production infrastructure. Thanks to the use of new research and drilling methods, for example, a new gas horizon in the Przemyśl deposit was discovered, until recently considered as a deposit in the end stage. It seemed that 64 billion m3 of gas extracted from a pool with identified resources 72 billion m3 was already exhausting as if this swimming pool, while on the basis of new seismic and deposit research it was estimated that the potential of the Przemyśl deposit could be increased by up to 20 billion m3. Let me remind you that domestic consumption in Poland, based on national extraction, is about 4 billion m3, so this 20 billion m3 is a really significant step forward. The company is planning further drillings, [[♪ The bell ♪]] which will allow to verify the potential of resources. This is a great success, thanks to which further exploitation of the deposit will be possible, including an increase in natural gas extraction.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-04-12-sejm-61-2.u90	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! To answer your questions, I would like to inform you that the Tourist Guarantee Fund was created by the Act of 22 July 2016 amending the Act on Tourism Services and the Act on Compulsory Insurance, Insurance and the Polish Office of Communication Insurances. It is a collateral supplement to the guarantee provided by the travel agency in the form of a bank or insurance guarantee agreement, as well as an insurance contract to customers or the receipt of payments from them to the trust account. The creation of the Tourist Guarantee Fund has increased the confidence of Polish tourists in national travel agencies, which contributed to a significant economic growth in the tourist event market in 2017. At the worst for Polish travel agencies of 2012, when insolvent announced 15 travel agencies, the amount of claims of all affected tourists amounted to more than PLN 50 million. In 2016, five travel agencies announced insolvency. Tourist claims against insolvent tourism organisers and tourist intermediaries resulting from contracts concluded before the date of entry into force of the Act which established the Tourist Guarantee Fund, i.e. Before 9 September 2016, they shall not be protected by the Tourist Guarantee Fund. In 2017, two travel agencies announced insolvency, but there was no need to mobilise funds from the Tourist Guarantee Fund, as the funds from the first pillar financial collateral were sufficient. According to the quarterly information provided in January 2018 by the Insurance Guarantee Fund at the end of 2017, payments of tourism organisers and tourism intermediaries for contributions to the Tourist Guarantee Fund amounted to 44 157 thousand. zł. It should be remembered that if only one of the 20 largest Polish travel agencies were to declare insolvency before the summer season or at the beginning of the summer season, this could result in claims of injured customers reaching at least tens of millions of zlotys. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-04-12-sejm-61-2.u97	Thank you very much. And please respond to the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Environment, Mr Sławomir Mazurka.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-04-12-sejm-61-2.u98	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, (PL) Mr President, Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, We have a very good time, because we have a committee meeting on the national air protection programme in parallel at the Chancellery of the President of the Council of Ministers. And I must say that under the hashtag, we can find the statement made by Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki at this meeting, in which the Prime Minister emphasized that with a great consequence we will be implementing the "Clean Air" programme, a programme to improve the quality of the environment. From this point on, the Prime Minister declared in his exposé that this is a government priority, a civilization challenge in which the Ministry of Environment will certainly play an active role in areas where it can help. I would also like to thank you for your declaration of cooperation in terms of improving the environment. I, too, will be based on information and on what the law protects us with regard to what the Member has said and my experience in this area, and I will resist the information of the regional environmental inspectors and the chief environmental officer. Of course, ladies and gentlemen, in Poland there is an air quality assessment and management system that complies with the requirements of EU law. The relevant national provisions are contained in the Environmental Law and in the implementing acts of that Law. Air quality assessments in the relevant distinguished zones are carried out annually by voivodship environmental inspectors. These assessments relate to the levels of substances in the air in a given zone compared to the previous year for substances such as PM10, suspended PM2,5 dust, sulphur dioxide, nitrogen dioxide, oxides of nitrogen, carbon, benzene, lead, cadmium, arsenic, nickel, benzo (a) pyrene and ozone, and then for each of these substances, the EVEN classify zones according to specific criteria. The results of these assessments for a given voivodship shall be communicated to the voivodship's board, which, in the event of a breach of standards, is required to develop an air protection programme. Such a program is an act of local law, is adopted by a resolution of the sejm of the voivodship. An integrated part of the air protection programme may be a short-term action plan, which is implemented in the event of a risk of exceeding not only the alert levels but also the level of information in terms of acceptable and target levels. E.g. The PM10 dust alarm level is 300 Sz/m3, the information level is 200 and the average daily limit is 50. These levels shall be set by the Minister for the Environment in agreement with the Minister for Health, by means of a regulation, in the light of the regulations adopted. Here, corrective action is aimed at alerting citizens against the threat and is already triggered in the event of a risk of exceeding the mid-day standard precisely mentioned 50. It should be noted that EU legislation provides for alert levels for the protection of health only for three substances: nitrogen dioxide, sulphur dioxide and ozone, and alert levels, in turn for plant protection reasons, for one substance: ozone, and no alert level for PM10 dust. For this substance, individual Member States individually set a level. Surely these levels, which are also advocated by NGOs, will be, are the subject of analysis. As I say, he works very actively for you... for this government's interministerial team. It is the first government to place such importance on air quality and compliance issues after 1989. With all the consequence, we will respect this right and, at the same time, in areas where it is still imperfect, we will certainly improve it. According to the 2016 air quality results, out of 40 zones in the country, air quality standards were exceeded in 35 PM10 dust zones, in 18 PM2.5 dust zones, and in 40 zones for benzo (a) pyrene and in four zones for nitrogen dioxide and in two zones for arsenic and in one zone for benzene. In the Podkarpackie voivodship, where the installation you mentioned is located, two zones were distinguished for the purpose of conducting an air quality test: the city of Rzeszów and the Podkarpacka zone. Overcoming the current air quality standards in 2016 concerned two pollutants: PM10 particulate matter and benzo (a) pyrene. ParlaMint-PL_2018-04-12-sejm-61-2.u202	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! I hope you come to our debate. In the draft act on social insurance for farmers and certain other laws, the government proposes to introduce into the national legal order a new type of civil law contract under the name: the aid agreement for harvests. During the legislative work in the committees, the social side drew attention to the urgent need to regulate social and economic matters for seasonal workers working on harvests. Farmers also pointed out that there were no hands to work on agricultural holdings for the harvest of agricultural products produced on their farms as part of their activities. We also learn from the impact assessment that this regulation will cover about 500,000. seasonal workers and 1400 thousand. agricultural holdings. It should be noted that a number of amendments have been made to the draft law in the course of its legislative work, which will certainly facilitate the application of the legislation adopted. This includes: extension of the delivery time for harvests from 120 days to 180 days. My club at first reading paid attention to this problem. I am pleased that this problem has been regulated during the committee work and that this deadline has been extended to 180 days. Another amendment adopted by the committee is the extension of the catalogue of operations and plants covered by the aid agreement for tobacco, hops, herbs and herbal plants. The Civic Platform proposes to include mushrooms in this catalogue. On this issue, we will make an appropriate amendment. We would like to point out that the implementation of the harvest aid agreement will require a compromise between the employer and the employer. In practice, this will also mean that on the farmer's side there will be new bureaucratic burdens related, firstly, to the calculation and payment of contributions: health, sickness, accident and maternity, and secondly, to the worker of the seasonal PIT document. At this point I would like to ask the Minister whether the administration, i.e. the Agricultural Social Insurance Fund and the National Health Fund, and farmers are prepared for this large operation in the field of handling, as well as the implementation of legislation, the action to be covered, as is apparent from the impact assessment, about 500,000. employees? At the same time, we are aware that farmers will bear the costs of applying the harvest aid agreement. And here I would ask the Minister to tell me what the burden will be on farmers or one seasonal worker employed. This is important in order to have a view of the additional costs the farmer will incur for the employment of a seasonal worker. We also point out that the Social Insurance Fund will deal with the issue of the incentive fund. The Civic Platform shall at this point propose an amendment to ensure that the incentive fund does not exceed 4% in the calendar year concerned. the planned expenditure of the component fund. Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! The modern amendment on this issue is aimed at ensuring that the fund's funding is not more than 4% the planned expenditure of the contribution fund. ParlaMint-PL_2018-04-12-sejm-61-2.u224	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I will try to answer as competently as possible all the questions that have been asked from the room, as well as during the club speeches, and address specific issues. Ladies and gentlemen! Many times there is a question of the divisibility and indivisibility of the contribution. This is a very important element that appears in the questions of representatives of practically every club. We tried to explain this during committee meetings during the process of this law. The contribution, which is to be removed as a helper at harvest, consists of two elements: so-called. health contributions and so-called "health contributions' the accident contribution. Health contribution is currently 142 PLN, the accident premium is 42 PLN, i.e. the total monthly amount of the burden on the farmer is 184 PLN. This is the only burden on the agricultural producer who will employ on his farm on the basis of a new form of civil law contract of a helper at harvest. As far as taxation is concerned, there is no additional income tax on the part of the agricultural producer. The income tax will be payable on the side of the helper at harvest, while the farmer will be obliged by the end of February each year to present to the tax office PIT-8C regarding the income of the helper at harvest. Of course, a helper with so-called collections. the national tax will be paid in our country, while in the case of helpers at harvests, for which this law is also created, e.g. from across the eastern border, they will pay this income tax in their country. On the other hand, with regard to this proportionality, this part of the accident, or that part of the 42 PLN, is calculated, of course, in proportion to the period of employment, so if this is the period of employment 3-, 7-, 10-day, this part will be calculated proportionally. On the other hand, as regards the health contribution, the health insurance contribution is monthly and indivisible. It shall be calculated on the basis of the assessment basis according to the title subject to health insurance in different ways. The amount of the contribution to the health insurance of households of farmers operating exclusively in the field of special divisions and farmers' assistants shall be 33.4%. the average monthly salary in the enterprise sector in the 4th quarter of the previous year, including income payments, announced by the President of the Central Statistical Office. The contribution from the new dimension base has been valid since 1 April of the year in question. The percentage of the health insurance contribution is 9% the basis of dimension, is monthly and indivisible. Therefore, it shall not be reduced in proportion when the person is subject to health insurance only for part of the month. This indivisibility does not apply only to seasonal workers, but it is generally accepted in our legislation, so this issue, I think, is clarified. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! There is a question of extending the catalogue of people who should perform so-called. seasonal work. Once again, the Directive is being invoked. Seasonal work usually occurs in sectors such as agriculture, gardening, especially during sowing or harvesting, or tourism, especially during holidays. It's clearly written down, which is seasonal work. These proposals for amendments result from close to an annual experience in the application of the rules. The proposed provisions, on the one hand, respond to the requests made by the applicants and to the opinion of the applicants for an opinion on the appropriateness of investments, in particular as regards the clarification of certain provisions, and clarify some questionable interpretation issues. The project will provide greater access to an opinion on the purpose of investment for medicinal products by extending the scope of healthcare services for which it is possible to submit an application. ParlaMint-PL_2018-04-12-sejm-61-2.u243	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! In listening to this discussion, I would like to address some of the comments, because I believe that they need to be corrected. Firstly, the instrument for assessing investment proposals in health care is an instrument that is already in place. This is not something new, something we are only introducing now, but it is an instrument which already operates under the Law of 21 July 2016 on the amendment of the Law on the provision of public-funded healthcare. We travelled all over Poland, met with every voivode and talked about the functioning of this instrument. The Voivodeships, as well as the starosts, i.e. local government units, told us that the instrument is a very good tool, but requires some modifications. As a result, the bill that we are investigating today is the answer to what we heard during meetings in the field with local government units, and is merely a clarification, ordering a certain state of affairs that already functions. I would like to remind you that a total of 1426 applications, including 213 to the Minister of Health and 1213 to the voivodships, have been submitted since the adoption of the Act in 2016. A total of 424 applications were rejected, i.e. 30%, of which 75% by the Minister of Health. A total of 978 opinions were delivered, including 134 issued by the Minister of Health. There were 833 positive opinions, of which the Minister of Health issued 100 positive opinions. In fact, the instrument mostly concerns local government units rather than central government supervised entities. There were 84 protests which resulted in 42 positive opinions and 24 negative opinions on these protests. I would like to point out that the European Commission has responded very positively to this instrument because, let me remind you, we have received money from European funds. The Commission wanted tools from us that would contribute to more efficient public spending. The Commission has taken the maps of health needs from us together with the IOWISZ evaluation instrument and expressed a positive position, so this is no attempt on democracy, this is not an attack on bodies, this is just an instrument to support rational public spending. [[Supporting instrument.]] It should also be noted that the contracting of benefits is regulated separately in the benefit law and has no relationship, i.e. it is not necessary to obtain an opinion from IOWISZ, and therefore it is not necessary to identify... This is something additional that affects the acquisition of the contract, but IOWISZ's assessment does not determine the acquisition of the contract by the entity. I will refer further to the fact that during the debate there was a statement that we are different actors. This particularly applies to entities supervised by the Minister for National Defence and the Minister for Home Affairs and Administration. I would like to remind you that we have a law of 25 May 2001 on the reconstruction and modernization of technical and financing of the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland. Therefore, in order not to interfere in the process of planning the development and modernisation of the Armed Forces on the basis of this law, we decided to exclude investments up to PLN 1 million for entities supervised by MON and MSWiA, but these entities must inform about such investments. Over 1 million these entities will have to obtain an opinion from IOWISZ. So we don't differentiate between entities, we just make a small exception not to interfere with the law on the security of modernization of the Armed Forces, which is justified from the state's point of view, because these entities, apart from normal contracting of benefits, also perform certain security and defence tasks. They must also make certain purchases related to security and defence and therefore this exception applies only to investment purchases up to PLN 1 million. This is not a total release, I remind you once again, it is up to PLN 1 million. Another thing: I would like to point out that the NFZ is a central payer who distributes money to central entities and local government entities. Therefore, it cannot be accepted that we intervene through IOWISZ in the local government, because it is really the payer who distributes contracts, distributes funds and must have an opinion about... he must know how public funds are spent. The bill, on which we are proceeding today, separates these two forms, is separately for re-investments, separately for new investments, which makes this score compatible with the investment in question. It's all about change. It's all about change. That's all. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-04-12-sejm-61-2.u247	Dear Marshal, Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, On behalf of the Minister for Investment and Development, I have the honour to present the Government's draft amendment to the Public-Private Partnership Act and some other laws. The aim of the Act is to eliminate the identified barriers to the further development of the public-private partnership market in Poland and, as a result, to increase the involvement of private capital in the implementation of investments and public services in Poland and to further improve their efficiency. The provisions of the proposed law will allow for the application of solutions known from international practice, which should further increase the attractiveness of PPP markets in Poland. The proposed law is the result of the Responsible Development Strategy for Public-Private Partnership and is one of the actions resulting from the implementation of the Resolution of the Council of Ministers of 26 July 2017 on the adoption of the Government Policy for the Development of Public-Private Partnership. Speaking of the Responsible Development Strategy, it should be pointed out that the current government is taking concrete steps to maintain the economic growth rate of the country, even if the available European funds are reduced and their acquisition becomes more difficult, for example. by using feedback instruments or instruments requested centrally by the European Commission. The proposed bill concerns amendments to the Public-Private Partnership Act and 19 other laws and aims to introduce legal solutions that will enable the efficient preparation and better implementation of infrastructure projects in the PPP formula. The project was widely consulted within an expert group composed of representatives of local governments, ministries, lawyers, PPP market practitioners and during inter-ministerial arrangements and public consultations. The comments and opinions submitted had a significant impact on the final shape of the bill. As a result of the most important planned changes to the regulations, any investment in Poland co-financed by the state budget of over PLN 300 million, but not co-financed by the European Union funds, will be obligatory, at an early stage of preparatory work, to be analysed in the PPP formula. PPP test. This analysis will then be the subject of an opinion by the Minister responsible for regional development. Any public contracting authority, regardless of the size of the planned investment in the PPP formula, will be able to ask the Minister responsible for regional development to give an opinion on the planned project, so-called. certification. There will also be an obligation to analyse the effectiveness of each planned PPP project. Tools will be put in place to facilitate market monitoring of PPP projects. There will be facilitation in the procedure for selecting a private partner. PPP projects on the basis of existing public entity companies will be possible. There will be an opportunity for PPP projects to be implemented by so-called. the subsidiary of a private partner who did not participate in the tender, thereby reducing the costs of the companies concerned in PPP projects. To facilitate the financing of PPP projects, e.g. as a result of the introduction of a clear basis for concluding the so-called. direct agreements and the introduction of so-called direct agreements. the rights of intervention. It will become permissible for local authorities to grant special grants to private partners to finance or finance investments related to the performance of their tasks. There will be an extension of the freedom of local authorities to increase parking fees in newly created urban areas of paid parking in 39 major cities in Poland as a result of an increase in the current limit for 15 years and the connection to the minimum wage. There will be a possibility for a private partner to build, renovate, maintain or equip social assistance homes. Expenditure on the implementation of these new tasks of the Minister responsible for regional development resulting from government policy adopted by the Council of Ministers for the development of public-private partnerships and the draft amendment of the Public-Private Partnership Act is estimated at less than PLN 17 million over 10 years, including a little over PLN 6 million for implementation of the so-called "Private Partnership Act." PPP test, of which for the year 2018 and 560 thousand. PLN and budgets for the next 9 years - after 645 thousand. PLN per year. PLN 10.5 million in the years 2018 to 2027 is planned to be spent within the limits allocated to the Minister responsible for regional development for voluntary certification of projects. If possible, expenditure for this purpose will be borne by European funds. The government's public-private partnership development policy assumes that 100 new public-private partnership agreements will be signed by 2020 with a value of 5% in the form of public-private partnership. investments in the national economy in the public sector, which should generate about PLN 23 billion. The increase in investment should translate into an increase in consumption, employment and additional revenue from goods and services tax. In addition, due to the amendment of the rules mentioned by me, which gives the possibility to increase the limit on parking fees in the centres of large cities, there will be an opportunity to obtain additional revenue by local government units, not the state budget. The amount of these revenues is not measurable, but at least 65% the proposed provisions will have to be used for investments to improve public transport in cities or green investments. Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-04-12-sejm-61-2.u249	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! It has been my honor to present to the High House a bill amending the Public-Private Partnership Act from print marked 2333. This draft consists of proposals for amendments to 19 laws from different branches of law. As the name says, it is the Public-Private Partnership Act. This is a very important, responsible law because it allows for close cooperation of private companies with public and public sector entities at business level. Even though it is already in force, although it is beneficial for all, this law is very unpopular. In this law, we must first of all draw up rules so that both the official and the entrepreneur have a clear and secure legal framework in which they can and should move without risk of conflict with the law. The main objective of this law is to bring about agreements consisting in the division of responsibilities and the financial burden of the public sector at the expense of a private entity, without exempting it from liability and supervision. In the case of such an agreement, the public sector makes investments without financial investment or with a very small symbolic effort, and the whole financial burden is taken over by the investor. In other words, it is the allocation of economic competence to a private entity, which means that it makes investments from its own financial resources, and an investor, the public sector, only accepts administrative, or supervisory powers. Maybe I'll give you an example, like the Minister. That's for example. the construction of a multi-storey parking lot with private funds, with its subsequent use by the investor for 30 years, which gives him reimbursement of the costs incurred. This law is very necessary and beneficial for both the state, public and economic sectors, as this law gives the public sector a chance to do so. A quick leap into the future, because it can make not only smaller but also multi-billion-dollar investments without engaging in its own resources, which gives a chance for a very large development, instantly explode investment, because the private sector has funds that are willing to invest under rational conditions, especially since, as we know, many municipalities are already on the verge of statutory debt. This law is also an opportunity for the Polish economy and Polish companies, because it opens new investment horizons to them, which consequently stimulates the economy, eliminates unemployment and, what's important, because they are mostly long-term investments, creates such a company so-called. positive economic stabilisation ballast. The objectives of the Act: to relieve the budgets of public entities in the investment phase by providing financing by private entities, so-called. external; ensuring better quality of services and the infrastructure provided, because, as we know, it will be in the interest of the contractor to make quality optimal, because it will be the controller for many years; the possibility of implementing innovative solutions for the construction and maintenance of individuals or the provision of various services; the identification and development of friendly rules for the use of the public-private partnership formula; the increase of public bodies' awareness of PPPs as an equivalent alternative method for providing high-quality infrastructure and public services. Models. There are two models. One is a concession contract for works and services - as you remember, I presented this law before the High Chamber - in which case the contractor receives remuneration from the recipients of services. And the other is a deal with the so-called. the availability fee for which the entity receives remuneration from a public entity, supplemented only by payments received from fees charged to the public. According to the justification of the bill, one such reason was the small scope of application of the Public-Private Partnership Act. Here, the risk associated with the application of the provisions of this law is too great. The next negative aspect relates to the conclusion of a contract by the company of a private partner's daughter against a potential opposition by a public entity which has to give its consent as there are no grounds for expressing or refusing it. In our view, such a refusal must be justified. A negative aspect is also the limitation of types of public-private partnership companies to capital companies. Further negative aspects are too far-reaching restrictions and lack of a mechanism allowing for a change in the duration of the company and a prolongation of the participation of a private partner. Such a situation of necessity may take place. Another negative aspect is the extension of the catalogue of cases in which the consent of all shareholders of a private partner's company will be required to the public entity in which the public-private partnership will be carried out, to the activities of the establishment of another company and to the acquisition or acquisition of shares or shares of another company. The need to obtain the consent of a public entity may result in such action being prevented and, consequently, may affect and impede the proper and timely execution of the investment. A negative consequence is also the contractual exclusion of the investor's joint and several liability towards subcontractors in respect of a public entity. This is apparent asymmetry in terms of public entities and private entities. Another negative aspect. The possibility of transferring all the rights and obligations of a private partner to a financing entity under an agreement between that entity and a public entity without the participation of a private partner in the absence of precise grounds for such transfer of rights may jeopardise any application of that measure to private partners where a serious threat, not specified, arises from circumstances for which they are not responsible or liable. And the most important negative consequence in our assessment, which may threaten the support of the Kukiz club15. The amendment of the Public Roads Act may mainly allow for increased parking fees in cities, and it seems unjustified, in our view, to carry out these changes within the framework of the non-binding amendment of the Public-Private Partnership Act. On behalf of the Kukiz Club15, I request that the project be referred to the local government committee. This committee is most appropriate to consider this project. Local authorities are most involved in the concept of public-private partnership. As a Kukiz club15, we do not apply for rejection at first reading, but we hope that what I have mentioned here will be corrected. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2018-04-12-sejm-61-2.u290	Thank you very much. The list of Members registered in the vote has been exhausted. Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Investment and Development Mr Witold Słowik will speak. You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-04-12-sejm-61-2.u291	Mr. Marshal! Dear Members, I am very pleased that the topic has aroused great interest in the fact that there are many questions, opinions. I will try to respond to all of them, as far as I can. In general, the questions included information or opinion that legislative changes themselves are not enough to develop public-private partnerships in Poland to a wider extent. Of course, I agree with this opinion, which is why the Ministry - Ministry of Development, and now the Ministry of Investment and Development - is conducting a number of other activities to develop this formula. From the very beginning, it has come to the topic as widely as possible, which is to say that we have first led to the provisions in the Responsible Development Strategy in the area: capital for development. There is a separate chapter on public-private partnership, indicating what needs to be done to promote public-private partnerships. There is also a strong emphasis on the fact that public-private partnerships are an equivalent source of funding for our country's development, at the same level as budget funding, grant funding, loans, capital markets or the European Fund for Strategic Investments. Thus, in the strategy paper of the highest rank, it is the expression of political will that public-private partnerships should be developed, that it is a mechanism that is clearly indicated, purposeful, that it is at the same level of transparency and transparency as contracts concluded under public procurement law. The next step was to create a government policy on the development of public-private partnerships. This document was adopted on 26 July. By joining this work, when the Ministry of Development became responsible for the development of the public-private partnership, we reactivated the public-private partnership team established by the Ministry of Economy, so that all these works would be carried out in the widest possible spectrum using the opinions of local authorities, other ministries, lawyers, public-private partnership market experts. This team worked in two groups. One group was involved in diagnosing legal barriers to the development of public-private partnerships. These legislative changes tabled are precisely the fruit of this group's work. Of course, then there was a wide public consultation, an inter-ministerial consultation. Because of, among other things, This consultation was so long because we started work less than two years ago in the summer of 2016. So there is no risk that these are actions without extensive consultation. We have also taken steps to promote public-private partnerships in order to erase the fourth one once and for all. According to the information I received from Prime Minister Morawiecki, we will replace the fourth prosecutor, the universality, so that the public-private partnership becomes universal. Another one of our actions and, by the way, an answer to one of our questions. As part of our activities, we have created a list of all potential public-private partnership projects in Poland. This list is published on our website. There are one hundred and fifty potential projects on it, several of which are already at the stage of proceedings. On the other hand, according to data from the end of the year, because we also conduct monitoring of existing, functioning public-private partnerships, there are 117 partnerships with global value: PLN 5.6 billion. Of course, after almost 10 years of operation of the law it is definitely unsatisfactory, hence our work. But that's all we're doing... We do other things, like. the development of contract standards so as to significantly increase the effectiveness of the proceedings being conducted, because at the moment, as I believe your Member has raised, only 116 of the four hundred and dozens of successful ones have ended, i.e. the effectiveness of 23%, so that this effectiveness will definitely increase, so that, at the start of the negotiations, the partners will of course have a non-obligatory model of the agreement, but they will be able to estimate the level of risks at the very beginning and more or less know what they are trying to do. Another important element, here the Member has said, is the certification of projects. Our department in the Ministry, the Department of Public-Private Partnership, will at the request of interested parties certify projects, i.e. giving them, in quotes, some kind of testimony that this project is economically effective, indicated for implementation in the form of a public-privatised partnership, and also, in quotes, a moral certificate, although I feel that the government itself kept a list of potential projects to be carried out in the form of a public-private partnership by the Ministry of Development, except that it is a kind of public sector offer for the private sector, also strengthens these projects, i.e. that the Ministry, that the government already knew about these projects. Another important element is education. Although we have been talking about public-private partnerships in Poland for several years, we have a current law from the turn of 2008 and 2009, this knowledge is insufficient, hence a broad training program. In fact, we trained ok. 1600 people, mostly local government officials, but we also organized two separate training courses for control units, for CBA, for the prosecution, for the accounting chambers, for NIK, for these negative historical connotations, so we are also trying to train control bodies, which have come very well prepared for this training. A few years ago, a report on public-private partnerships in Poland was created. This report by NIK did not confirm these stereotypes that most public-private partnerships are of some criminal, corrupt nature. According to this report, the number of irregularities in public-private partnership contracts is in no case greater than in public procurement. So here is a more mental barrier resulting from the practices of the 1990s, where every action at the interface of a public entity and a private entity, often inappropriate action, in a common sense was a public-private partnership, but it was not a public-private partnership within the statutory meaning. Therefore, we must decisively break these negative stereotypes and certainly legislative action also serves this purpose, but this is just one of the areas of our broad action. However, there was another question about what these legislative changes are intended to serve and what is our ultimate goal. In the government's policy on the development of public-private partnerships, four ambitious objectives have been defined very closely. The first target is 100 new public-private partnerships by 2020. The second objective is to launch at least 10 government-private partnerships. The next goal, the most ambitious of them all, is 5%. public expenditure in the public-private partnership formula. The fourth objective is to improve the efficiency of the proceedings. Right now, like I said, only twenty-six percent, about 23%. It ends with a deal. In line with the objectives set by the government's public-private partnership policy, 40% procedures are expected to be successful, so these objectives are very ambitious. One of the necessary conditions, but of course insufficient, to achieve these ambitious objectives is to eliminate legal barriers. This legislation is precisely designed to eliminate barriers to the development of public-private partnerships identified by the broadly understood market. There was also a question here about the parking lot that was most interested in public opinion. So I wanted to stress, first of all, that under no circumstances does the government raise parking fees and there are no such proposals under these legislative proposals. It is to give local governments the right to raise parking fees so that local authorities can effectively fight smog and that the creation of parking spaces in the public-private partnership formula is economically viable. This applies only to the 39 largest Polish cities and only to inner-urban zones, zones specifically created for the purposes of these statutory provisions and the connection of the maximum permissible amount with the minimum remuneration. According to the proposals, this is 0.45% the lowest salary for the first hour of parking. Another issue, the link between the Public Procurement Law and the Public-Private Partnership Act and the concession law. We treat these three laws as the basis for the development of public-private partnerships in Poland and we want them to be compatible with one another and that the provisions of the Public Procurement Law, which regulate the same legal areas, are similar to those of the Public-private Partnership Act and the Public-Private Partnership Act. In addition, a new concession law has been adopted in recent years in the framework of the implementation of the EU Directive and the work on this law has been aimed at ensuring that these provisions are fully compatible with each other. It's a matter of law of intervention. This is about the possibility - I am using a thought shortcut and I am talking colloquially - of saving certain vulnerable public-private partnerships because public-private partnerships are largely implemented on the basis of bank debt financing. So we want to create such a mechanism so that when a private entity has problems, it does not implement the contract, the entity that lends it is able to enter into the rights and obligations of a private partner. A number of actions, as the rapporteur has pointed out, are aimed at making public-private partnership provisions more flexible so that it can be brought to a happy end at the stage of the procedure. Hence the possibility of ceding rights on a daughter's company. One of the questions was asked. [[♪ The bell ♪]] On purpose.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-04-12-sejm-61-2.u292	Thank you very much, Minister. The discussion is closed. The Law on the System of General Courts and the Law of 8 December 2017 on the Supreme Court (prints Nos 2389 and 2441). I ask Mr Bartłomiej Wróblewski to submit a report to the committee.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-04-12-sejm-61-2.u293	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! I present on behalf of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights a report on the draft law amending the Law of 27 July 2001. The Law on the System of General Courts and the Act of 8 December 2017 on the Supreme Court. These are 2389 and 2441 prints. The Sejm at its 61st meeting on 11 April 2018 pursuant to Article 7 (1) of the Rules of Procedure. 39 (1) (a) 2 Rules of Procedure of the Sejm referred this draft law to the Committee on Justice and Human Rights for consideration. The committee held its meeting on the same day, i.e. 11 April 2018 The meeting was attended by Deputy Director of the Legislative Department at the Ministry of Justice Wojciech Ulitko. The project introduces a two-step consultation procedure when the presidents and vice-presidents of the courts are dismissed and objective criteria of the interest of the judiciary and an important social interest as a basis for the National Court of Justice's consent to further judgment by the judge after the age of 65, and gives the President of the Republic of Poland the power to object to further ruling by the Supreme Court judge after the age of 65, as well as the competence to appoint assessors. In addition, the project balances the age of retirement - 65 years - of judges of women and judges of men, while at the same time leaves the right for judges of women to choose to retire after the age of 60, regardless of the period of employment. Finally, the draft regulates the convening of the first meeting of the National Courts after the President's resignation. During the committee meeting, all the regulations of the project were systematically discussed. Four substantive amendments and numerous legislative amendments were adopted. The substantive amendments differentiate the procedure for remaining a judge after reaching the age of 65 in the case of the general courts and the Supreme Court, as well as introducing a reserve procedure for convening the first meeting of the National Courts following the release of the President's office, in the event that the first President of the Supreme Court has not convened the meeting. The discussion of the project was lively and even turbulent at times. The doubts raised will probably be reflected in the amendments tabled at the time of this reading. After a few hours of work, the project was adopted by the committee. 11 Members voted in favour, 7 opposed, 2 abstained. Therefore, the Committee on Justice and Human Rights, after considering the draft law, asks the High Sejm to adopt the draft law as proposed in the report of the Committee on Printing No 2441. I would like to thank the ladies and gentlemen and all the members of the committee for their cooperation. In the event of specific questions, I shall remain at the disposal of the High House. ParlaMint-PL_2018-04-12-sejm-61-2.u295	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! The European legal system is based on the recognition of constitutional pluralism in Article. 4 of the Treaty on European Union, which at the same time guarantees that each Member State can shape its judicial system in a sovereign way. The tensions between the legislative, executive and judicial authorities are enshrined in the nature of the democratic system, so their existence does not mean that the independence of judges is at stake. The Treaty on European Union secures the national identity of the Member States as the exclusive national competence, which means that judicial reforms should be assessed at national level by the competent authorities. Polish judges have very strong guarantees of this independence, one of the strongest in Europe. This law, which I called the democratic package for the judiciary yesterday, we want to strengthen these guarantees of independence and independence even more. That is why we are now proposing, as well as yesterday, further amendments to the Committee on Justice and Human Rights. These committee meetings were requested by the High Committee. The first was intended to complement the mechanism for expressing opinions and to make the system more coherent with regard to the selection of the College of District Courts and Appeals. The second amendment, tabled yesterday at the committee's meeting, provided that the power to agree to the continued office of the judge after he reached his retirement age, should be transferred to the National Judicial Council with the exception of other bodies such as the Minister of Justice or the President of the Republic. Since, in accordance with the amendment, the body set up by the Constitution to uphold the independence of the courts and the independence of the judges, its decisions should be final. This is in line with the assumptions underlying the original version of the proposed law, according to which the KRS was to decide on appeals against the President's provisions. The exclusion of the President from the whole procedure leaves all the powers in this respect to a collegial body composed by a vast majority of representatives of the judicial community. There is therefore no need for another appeal from his decision. The next amendment, tabled yesterday as the fourth, was intended to replace the procedure for the President's consent to continue to serve as a Supreme Court Judge after the age of 65 with the possibility of objecting to the further occupation of that position by a judge who declared his will and presented a medical certificate. The purpose of the last amendment, tabled as the third amendment, is among others. supplementing the rules on the convening of the first meeting of the National Judicial Council after the resignation of the chair of the Council in such a way as to ensure the effective convening of that meeting. Enough with the obstruction! The Polish state will operate, the Polish judiciary will be reformed, and the theoretical state you created no longer exists. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2018-04-12-sejm-61-2.u304	Mr Ireneusz Gaina, the Free and Solidarnia Members' Circle, will now speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2018-04-12-sejm-61-2.u305	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Circle of Members of the Free and Solidarnia, I present my position on the report of the Committee on Justice and Human Rights on the draft law amending the Law on the Law on the System of General Courts, the Act on the National Council of Courts and the Act on the Supreme Court, prints Nos 2389 and 2441. The draft law submitted concerns amendments to the three laws which are crucial for the proper functioning of the judiciary. After the implementation of the changes in the functioning of the justice system, it is clear that some of them already have positive effects. We are talking about implementing the principle of random assignment of cases or strengthening the principle of independence of judges by prohibiting the transfer of judges to another department without their consent. The effects of the remaining changes should still be waited for, but for now it can be said that the direction chosen is the right one. Applicants propose to adopt new rules in several areas. Firstly, a two-step consultation procedure is envisaged when the Presidents and Vice-Presidents of the Court are dismissed with the participation of the College of the competent court and the National Council of Courts. It is worth noting, however, that if applicants, as indicated in the explanatory memorandum, are concerned by the two-step nature of this procedure, they should clarify in the wording of the Act that, after a negative opinion has been expressed by the College of the Court, the Minister of Justice shall submit to the National Council of the Court of Justice his intention to dismiss the President or Vice-President of the Court for an opinion, rather than, as is included in the draft law, he may present. Otherwise, the second degree of procedure may be illusory, entirely dependent on the discretionary decision of the Minister of Justice. As explained by the justification, this was not the intention of the applicants. Secondly, applicants shall amend the procedure for consenting to further judgment by a judge of the General Court after he has reached 65 years of age. The objective criteria of the interest of the judiciary and of an important social interest were applied as a basis for the judge's consent to further judgment after the age of 65, as well as the power to express that consent to the constitutional body, the National Judicial Council. In the case of the Law on the Supreme Court in respect of judges ending 65 years, the draft provides for a revised procedure for consenting to further ruling on a conditional obligation for the judge to make a declaration of his willingness to continue to occupy his post and to provide a certificate stating that he is capable of performing his duties as a judge and that he is not opposed by the President of the Republic of Poland. Thirdly, the bill provides, in accordance with the recommendations of the European Commission, for the standard age to be equal to 65 years of retirement of judges of common courts for men and women, while allowing judges of women to choose to retire after the age of 60. Fourthly, it is extremely important, in terms of the smooth functioning of the constitutional organs of the State, that the amendment contained in the draft on which the Committee on Justice and Human Rights was working is an amendment to the Act on the National Council of Courts concerning the convening of the first meeting of the Council of the new term of office. The Commission has also proposed a solution for the Members of the Free and Solidarnia Circle. We are talking about an alternative path to convening the first sitting when it is not possible for any reason to convene this meeting by the first President of the Supreme Court. The first meeting may then be convened by the oldest member of the council who is a judge or a retired judge, possibly as stated in the article added. Article 20 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 5, the next oldest members of the council being judges or retired judges. This is an optimal solution, practiced in the event of the first meetings of the bodies of many other institutions when the oldest member of the body does so. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In the United States, a judge caught stealing his neighbor's underwear was arrested, carried out in handcuffs, condemned and thrown out of the state of justice as the one who permanently and irretrievably lost his qualifications for ruling. And in Poland the Supreme Court against a judge caught stealing parts from a drill has ruled a disciplinary penalty reducing his remuneration by 20%. Doesn't that judge lose his jurisdiction? ParlaMint-PL_2018-04-12-sejm-61-2.u339	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present a draft of the government's novel on personal and corporate income tax. This is a novel which, in principle, gives additional privileges to taxpayers related to their settlement for the current year, and this is quite a point change which, it seems, is needed, perhaps necessary and concerns a number of cases. First of all, we amend the PIT law to include depreciation write-offs in the tax costs from the donations acquired and the decline of fixed assets and intangible assets. This change is a solution to the demands of family businesses, which have recognised that certain provisions of the current legal situation are asymmetrical and require a certain level playing field. The second topic discussed in the novel, the second amendment is the redrafting and extension of the catalogue of activities entitled to the costs of obtaining 50% revenue, so-called. contract for work with the transfer of copyright. The vast majority of these additional privileges seem to have been interpreted from the existing legal situation, but it also seems that by proceeding over this novel, part of these interpretations can be eliminated, simply writing a wider catalogue of these professions. Another amendment is the introduction in the PIT Act of tax exemption of income from aid financed from the Police. This is a change quite obvious and consistent with similar records for other services. We have changes in the taxation of winnings earned in slots, card games, dice games, cylindrical games run by legitimate players. It is also a change that evens out the tax burden of games or winnings for a number of types of games. We have an amendment to the PIT Act on the regulation of taxation of income obtained from the disposal of shares acquired under the so-called. incentive schemes. It also seems that today's legal situation can also be interpreted in this spirit, but in response to the concerns of the business community and advisors, we clarify this provision. Another very important and expected change is called. exemption in the PIT Act and in the CIT Act on old loans surrendered, so-called An old wallet. This is a change expected by many people who benefit from cooperative housing, which is expected to have old loans cancelled this year, which in today's legal situation could result in taxation of these values. This change is quite important financially and I will say more about the budgetary consequences of this change. The next area that we are modifying includes a change in the wording of the CIT Act on tax consequences related to the contribution to the company. Again, it seems to be a little bit of a rule that can be interpreted from today's legal situation, but we do decide, despite the interpretation already given this year, to this change, so as to overcome any speculation and unnecessary questioning of our position. The draft law also contains a novelty of the provisions concerning the minimum property tax. The current legislation has been brought forward with a complaint to the European Commission, after clarification from our side on the purpose of these provisions, the European Commission has recognised that our provisions are in their essential part justified and correct, while we have come to the findings of modifying these provisions for both the current year and 2019, which in principle seal the specific mechanism and make it even better for this aggressive optimization, which has stood at the beginning, for the preparation of these provisions at the beginning. These changes, which are agreed upon and included in this project, tend to extend the catalogue of real estate covered by this special tax. It seems that from our perspective, the primary coverage of commercial facilities and office buildings with this tax was sufficient because we saw there the most common manifestations of aggressive optimization. It seemed to us that this is the correct mechanism for addressing this problem or preventing this problem, but nothing prevents, according to the perspective of the European Commission, the treatment of all types of real estate equally covered by this minimum tax also other real estate, while maintaining a very high threshold, as we believe, of PLN 10 million concerning the value of capital invested in those properties, which is still causing this minimum property tax obligation. We are also introducing such a new institution, a procedure that causes the minimum tax to actually fall into this need to reduce aggressive optimization, but allows taxpayers who are transparent to act in an objective and market manner to avoid the negative effects of this minimum tax. It is a very fair and quite clear mechanism. If, colloquially speaking, the taxpayer has overinvested and this is evidently due to the financing agreements it has concluded, its contracts to achieve the revenue from the rental of the property, if it objectively presents to us its tax situation, which will generate a tax lower than that resulting from the mechanism of the special minimum tax, then we will of course recognise this right and this minimum tax will not apply and will be refunded. We are also introducing a clause that is professionally called GAAR, a clause that makes it difficult to circumvent these new regulations so that they can act preventively and pro-budgetally. So that I present this draft to the High House. Of course, we're waiting for questions. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2018-04-12-sejm-61-2.u341	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice to present a position vis-à-vis the Government's draft law amending the Personal Income Tax Act, the Corporate Income Tax Act and the Flat-rate Income Tax Act on certain income generated by individuals, prints No 2291 and 2291-A. The amendments introduced primarily modify the regulations which began to apply from 1 January 2018 on the basis of the amendments made to that Act in October 2017, in respect of fixed assets and intangible assets acquired by inheritance or donations. In the event of acquisition by means of a fall in fixed assets and intangible assets, the Act shall revert to the rules in force before the date of entry into force of that amendment. On the other hand, in the case of funds acquired through donations, the project introduces the principle of continuation by the gifted depreciation write-offs made by the donor. This means that the gifted will not be deprived of the possibility of making depreciation copies, but will accept them in the value previously defined by the donor. The bill also provides for the exemption of income obtained from financial assistance received from the Police by persons protected by it under the Law on the Crown Witness and the Act on Protection and Aid to the Victim and the Witness. The exemption will also cover other similar benefits, which are paid in connection with the so-called benefits. the institution of a small witness as described in the Penal Code Act, the application of which often generates the need to protect and assist the beneficiary. The government in this amendment also wants to address the problem of economic and systemic transformation related to the market share of housing loans from the 1990s. It therefore adds in the PIT and CIT Act an exemption from taxation of an amount covering the remission of debt related to the existing housing loan under the State aid law. Such a solution will help to clarify the issue of loans so-called. An old wallet. The bill will also ensure uniform treatment of participants of legal games on slots, card games, dice, cylindrical games and bingo games regardless of the player who organizes the game. According to the proposed solutions, the total income tax exemption from natural persons will be subject to winnings in the above mentioned. games. The amendment to the PIT Act also includes a change that cleans and adds areas for which 50% of revenue costs can be applied. In view of the fact that the added provision raised doubts as to interpretation among the creative professions presented, it was proposed to redefine and add areas in the field of construction engineering, translation and computer games. The draft amendment to the CIT Act proposes provisions that clearly indicate that the tax is not subject to financial and non-monetary contributions made to companies. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! ParlaMint-PL_2018-04-12-sejm-61-2.u369	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Committee on Foreign Affairs on the Government's draft law on the termination of the Agreement between the Government of the Polish People's Republic and the Government of the French Republic on the promotion and mutual protection of investment, signed in Paris on 14 February 1989. The need to terminate the promotion and mutual protection agreements concluded between the Member States of the European Union is the result of the European Commission's questioning these agreements as incompatible with Union law. The European Commission is therefore considering the initiation of formal proceedings against Poland concerning the non-compliance of such agreements with European Union law. Such formal proceedings of the European Commission are already taking place against countries such as the Netherlands, Austria, Slovakia, Sweden and Romania. For this reason, it is appropriate for Poland to endeavour to terminate this agreement on the basis of a bilateral agreement which would provide for the waiver of the extended term of the agreement. The French Republic is currently not interested in the termination of the above-mentioned agreement with the Republic of Poland, including the above-mentioned clause, so it is appropriate to terminate the agreement in the opinion of the Republic of Poland. According to Art. The contract may be terminated at any time by each Contracting Party with a notice period of 12 months after the expiry of the initial period of 10 years which expired on 10 February 2000. And on the basis of art. 12 investments made prior to the termination of the contract will be protected under that agreement for 15 years after the termination of the contract. At the same time, the Republic of Poland will continue its consultation with the French Republic in order to bring the agreement to an end, together with an extension clause. If such consent is obtained prior to the entry into force of the termination of the Agreement, the Republic of Poland will take action to terminate the Agreement. The termination of the agreement should not have political consequences. The Member States of the European Union, including the Republic of Poland and the French Republic, have been invited by the European Commission to conclude the intra-EU agreements. The termination of the agreement will also have no negative social consequences, on the contrary, it should be expected that it will be welcomed by public criticism in Poland of intra-EU agreements and international investment arbitration. The termination of the agreement should also not affect economic relations between Poland and the French Republic. The agreement was concluded at a time when the Republic of Poland was not a member of the European Union. This agreement was intended to attract French investments to Poland. Currently, the level of legislation and access to the general judiciary in the Republic of Poland provide foreign investors with a guarantee of the protection of investments with the possibility to enforce their rights on a judicial basis. Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! The Committee on Foreign Affairs, following its first reading and the examination of this draft law at its meeting on 22 March 2018, adopted unanimously a report requesting the adoption by the High Sejm of a draft law in print no. 2282 without amendments. Thank you.

The Court of Justice of the European Union also expressed its opinion on this issue, which, in response to the question referred for a preliminary ruling, stated that the investment promotion and protection agreements were incompatible with Union law. It should also be noted that the conclusion of the agreement took place in a different socio-economic reality. The protection of investment through international arbitration was intended to be a kind of fuse for a foreign investor in an uncertain period of economic transformation. The European Commission has serious concerns that agreements for the promotion and mutual protection of investment, including this agreement and their individual provisions, are in line with or may conflict with the provisions of the Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union, in particular the principles of freedom of movement of capital, freedom of establishment and freedom to provide services, and the fundamental principles of European Union law. The European Commission also has reservations about the settlement of investment disputes in bilateral investment agreements, which, according to the European Commission, seems to be contrary to the exclusive competence of the courts of the European Union. The termination of the agreement will lead to compliance with European Union law of Polish-French economic relations. At Community level, the termination of the agreement should not have political consequences. As we read in the explanatory memorandum, there should also be no negative social and economic impact. ParlaMint-PL_2018-04-12-sejm-61-2.u493	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the Law and Justice Club to present a position on the draft law amending the Law on Government Departments and the Law on Infrastructure for Spatial Information, print no. 2392. The draft law provides for a change in the form of the transfer of infrastructure and spatial information from the section: IT to the section: construction, spatial planning and development and housing. As a result, the Minister responsible for construction, planning and spatial planning and housing will be equipped with a tool to obtain the necessary information for investment purposes. The infrastructure of spatial information shall provide the necessary geoinformation. It is reasonable for it to be supervised and coordinated by the Minister responsible for construction, planning and spatial planning and housing. On the other hand, changes in spatial information infrastructure assume that the creation and maintenance of geoportal spatial information will be the responsibility of the main surveyor of the country. The bill also regulates issues such as: take over cases initiated from 1 January 2017 until the date of entry into force of the Act and provide relevant documentation. Changes related to the transfer of spatial information infrastructure to the Ministry of Investment and Development will allow to focus all instruments in one resort, i.e. geodesia, cartography, planning and spatial planning, infrastructure of spatial information necessary for the establishment of a government geoinformation service centre. The Law and Justice Club fully accepts the draft law presented in print no. 2392. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
